Leaders in the formation of the United States
"Founding Fathers" redirects here. For the founding fathers of other countries, see [List of national founders](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_national_founders "List of national founders").
Founding Fathers of the United States  
---  
1760s–1820s  
[![](https://upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/f/f9/Declaration_of_Independence_%281819%29%2C_by_John_Trumbull.jpg/250px-Declaration_of_Independence_%281819%29%2C_by_John_Trumbull.jpg)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:Declaration_of_Independence_\(1819\),_by_John_Trumbull.jpg)The [Committee of Five](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Committee_of_Five "Committee of Five") ([Adams](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/John_Adams "John Adams"), [Livingston](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Robert_R._Livingston "Robert R. Livingston"), [Sherman](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Roger_Sherman "Roger Sherman"), [Jefferson](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Thomas_Jefferson "Thomas Jefferson"), and [Franklin](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Benjamin_Franklin "Benjamin Franklin")) present their draft of the [Declaration of Independence](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/United_States_Declaration_of_Independence "United States Declaration of Independence") to the [Second Continental Congress](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Second_Continental_Congress "Second Continental Congress") in Philadelphia on June 28, 1776, as depicted in [John Trumbull](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/John_Trumbull "John Trumbull")'s [1818 portrait](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Declaration_of_Independence_\(painting\) "Declaration of Independence \(painting\)").  
Location | [Thirteen Colonies](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Thirteen_Colonies "Thirteen Colonies")  
Including | Signers of the [Declaration of Independence](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/United_States_Declaration_of_Independence "United States Declaration of Independence") (1776), [Articles of Confederation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Articles_of_Confederation "Articles of Confederation") (1781), and [United States Constitution](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Constitution_of_the_United_States "Constitution of the United States") (1789)  
Leaders | 
  * [John Adams](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/John_Adams "John Adams")
  * [Samuel Adams](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Samuel_Adams "Samuel Adams")
  * [Benjamin Franklin](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Benjamin_Franklin "Benjamin Franklin")

[![](https://upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/b/b6/Gilbert_Stuart_Williamstown_Portrait_of_George_Washington.jpg/250px-Gilbert_Stuart_Williamstown_Portrait_of_George_Washington.jpg)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:Gilbert_Stuart_Williamstown_Portrait_of_George_Washington.jpg)[George Washington](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/George_Washington "George Washington"), a key Founding Father, was commanding general of the [Continental Army](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Continental_Army "Continental Army") during the [Revolutionary War](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/American_Revolutionary_War "American Revolutionary War") and a Revolutionary hero, presided over the [Constitutional Convention](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Constitutional_Convention_\(United_States\) "Constitutional Convention \(United States\)") and became the nation's first president in April 1789.[[1]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Founding_Fathers_of_the_United_States#cite_note-1)
The **Founding Fathers of the United States** , referred to as the **Founding Fathers** or the **Founders** by Americans, were a group of late-eighteenth-century [American revolutionary](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/American_Revolution "American Revolution") leaders who [united](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/United_Colonies "United Colonies") the [Thirteen Colonies](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Thirteen_Colonies "Thirteen Colonies"), oversaw the [War of Independence](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/American_Revolutionary_War "American Revolutionary War") from [Great Britain](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kingdom_of_Great_Britain "Kingdom of Great Britain"), established the [United States of America](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/United_States "United States"), and crafted a [framework of government](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Constitution_of_the_United_States "Constitution of the United States") for the new nation. 
The Founding Fathers include those who wrote and signed the [United States Declaration of Independence](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/United_States_Declaration_of_Independence "United States Declaration of Independence"), the [Articles of Confederation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Articles_of_Confederation "Articles of Confederation"), and the [Constitution of the United States](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Constitution_of_the_United_States "Constitution of the United States"), certain military personnel who fought in the [American Revolutionary War](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/American_Revolutionary_War "American Revolutionary War"), and others who greatly assisted in the nation's formation. The single person most identified as Father of the United States is [George Washington](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/George_Washington "George Washington"), commanding general in the American Revolution and the nation's first [president](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/President_of_the_United_States "President of the United States"). In 1973, the historian [Richard B. Morris](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Richard_B._Morris "Richard B. Morris") identified seven figures as key founders, based on what he called the "triple tests" of leadership, longevity, and statesmanship: [John Adams](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/John_Adams "John Adams"), [Benjamin Franklin](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Benjamin_Franklin "Benjamin Franklin"), [Alexander Hamilton](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Alexander_Hamilton "Alexander Hamilton"), [John Jay](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/John_Jay "John Jay"), [Thomas Jefferson](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Thomas_Jefferson "Thomas Jefferson"), [James Madison](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/James_Madison "James Madison"), and Washington.[[2]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Founding_Fathers_of_the_United_States#cite_note-morris1-2)
Most of the Founding Fathers had ancestry traceable back to [England](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/England "England"), though many had family roots extending across the other regions of the [British Isles](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/British_Isles "British Isles"): [Scotland](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Scotland "Scotland"), [Wales](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wales "Wales"), the [Caribbean](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Charlestown,_Nevis "Charlestown, Nevis")'s, and [Ireland](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ireland "Ireland"). Additionally, some traced their lineage back to the early Dutch settlers of New York ([New Netherland](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/New_Netherland "New Netherland")) during the colonial era, while others were descendants of French [Huguenots](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Huguenots "Huguenots") who settled in the British [Thirteen Colonies](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Thirteen_Colonies "Thirteen Colonies"), escaping religious persecution in [France](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/France "France").[[3]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Founding_Fathers_of_the_United_States#cite_note-English_Emigration-3)[[4]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Founding_Fathers_of_the_United_States#cite_note-4)[[5]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Founding_Fathers_of_the_United_States#cite_note-5) Many of them were wealthy merchants, lawyers, landowners, and [slaveowners](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Slavery_in_the_United_States "Slavery in the United States"). 
## Historical founders
[[edit](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Founding_Fathers_of_the_United_States&action=edit&section=1 "Edit section: Historical founders")]
[![](https://upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/b/b1/Official_Presidential_portrait_of_Thomas_Jefferson_%28by_Rembrandt_Peale%2C_1800%29%28cropped%29.jpg/250px-Official_Presidential_portrait_of_Thomas_Jefferson_%28by_Rembrandt_Peale%2C_1800%29%28cropped%29.jpg)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:Official_Presidential_portrait_of_Thomas_Jefferson_\(by_Rembrandt_Peale,_1800\)\(cropped\).jpg)[Thomas Jefferson](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Thomas_Jefferson "Thomas Jefferson"), a key Founding Father, was the primary author of the [Declaration of Independence](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/United_States_Declaration_of_Independence "United States Declaration of Independence"), which [Pulitzer Prize](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pulitzer_Prize_for_History "Pulitzer Prize for History")-winning historian [Joseph Ellis](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Joseph_Ellis "Joseph Ellis") says contains "the most potent and consequential words in U.S. history".[[6]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Founding_Fathers_of_the_United_States#cite_note-American_Creation-6)
The historian [Richard B. Morris](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Richard_B._Morris "Richard B. Morris")'s selection of seven key founders was widely accepted through the 20th century.[[7]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Founding_Fathers_of_the_United_States#cite_note-7)[[8]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Founding_Fathers_of_the_United_States#cite_note-HarvardFounders-8) John Adams, Thomas Jefferson, and Benjamin Franklin were members of the [Committee of Five](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Committee_of_Five "Committee of Five") that were charged by the [Second Continental Congress](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Second_Continental_Congress "Second Continental Congress") with drafting the [Declaration of Independence](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/United_States_Declaration_of_Independence "United States Declaration of Independence"). Franklin, Adams, and John Jay negotiated the 1783 [Treaty of Paris](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Treaty_of_Paris_\(1783\) "Treaty of Paris \(1783\)"), which established American independence and brought an end to the [American Revolutionary War](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/American_Revolutionary_War "American Revolutionary War").[[9]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Founding_Fathers_of_the_United_States#cite_note-9) The constitutions drafted by Jay and Adams for their respective states of [New York (1777)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Constitution_of_New_York "Constitution of New York") and [Massachusetts (1780)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Constitution_of_Massachusetts "Constitution of Massachusetts") proved influential in the language used in developing the U.S. Constitution.[[10]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Founding_Fathers_of_the_United_States#cite_note-10)[[11]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Founding_Fathers_of_the_United_States#cite_note-11)[[12]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Founding_Fathers_of_the_United_States#cite_note-12) _[The Federalist Papers](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Federalist_Papers "The Federalist Papers")_ , which advocated the ratification of the [Constitution](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Constitution_of_the_United_States "Constitution of the United States"), were written by Alexander Hamilton, James Madison, and Jay. George Washington was [commander-in-chief](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Commander-in-chief "Commander-in-chief") of the [Continental Army](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Continental_Army "Continental Army") and later president of the [Constitutional Convention](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Constitutional_Convention_\(United_States\) "Constitutional Convention \(United States\)").[[13]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Founding_Fathers_of_the_United_States#cite_note-13)[[14]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Founding_Fathers_of_the_United_States#cite_note-Jilson_p._291-14)
Each of these men held additional important roles in the early government of the United States. Washington, Adams, Jefferson, and Madison served as the first four presidents; Adams and Jefferson were the nation's first two vice presidents;[[15]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Founding_Fathers_of_the_United_States#cite_note-15) Jay was the nation's first [chief justice](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chief_Justice_of_the_United_States "Chief Justice of the United States");[[16]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Founding_Fathers_of_the_United_States#cite_note-16) Hamilton was the first [secretary of the treasury](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/United_States_Secretary_of_the_Treasury "United States Secretary of the Treasury");[[17]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Founding_Fathers_of_the_United_States#cite_note-17) Jefferson was the first [secretary of state](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/United_States_Secretary_of_State "United States Secretary of State");[[18]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Founding_Fathers_of_the_United_States#cite_note-18)[[19]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Founding_Fathers_of_the_United_States#cite_note-19) and Franklin was America's most senior diplomat from the start of the Revolutionary War through its conclusion with the signing of the Treaty of Paris in 1783.[[20]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Founding_Fathers_of_the_United_States#cite_note-20)
The list of Founding Fathers is often expanded to include the [signers of the Declaration of Independence](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Signing_of_the_United_States_Declaration_of_Independence "Signing of the United States Declaration of Independence") and individuals who later approved the [U.S. Constitution](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Constitution_of_the_United_States "Constitution of the United States").[[2]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Founding_Fathers_of_the_United_States#cite_note-morris1-2) Some scholars regard all delegates to the [Constitutional Convention](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Constitutional_Convention_\(United_States\) "Constitutional Convention \(United States\)") as Founding Fathers whether they approved the Constitution or not.[[21]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Founding_Fathers_of_the_United_States#cite_note-ArchivesDeclaration-21)[[22]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Founding_Fathers_of_the_United_States#cite_note-ArchivesFramers-22) In addition, some historians include signers of the [Articles of Confederation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Articles_of_Confederation "Articles of Confederation"), which was adopted in 1781 as the nation's first constitution.[[23]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Founding_Fathers_of_the_United_States#cite_note-Padover-23)
Historians have come to recognize others as founders, such as [Revolutionary War military leaders](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_military_leaders_in_the_American_Revolutionary_War "List of military leaders in the American Revolutionary War") as well as participants in developments leading up to the war, including prominent writers, orators, and other men and women who contributed to cause.[[8]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Founding_Fathers_of_the_United_States#cite_note-HarvardFounders-8)[[24]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Founding_Fathers_of_the_United_States#cite_note-24)[[25]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Founding_Fathers_of_the_United_States#cite_note-Beck-25)[[26]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Founding_Fathers_of_the_United_States#cite_note-26) Since the 19th century, the Founding Fathers have shifted from the concept of them as [demigods](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Demigods "Demigods") who created the modern nation-state, to take into account their inability to address issues such as [slavery](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Slavery_in_the_United_States "Slavery in the United States") and the debt owed after the American Revolutionary War.[[27]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Founding_Fathers_of_the_United_States#cite_note-27)[[28]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Founding_Fathers_of_the_United_States#cite_note-28) Scholars emphasize that the Founding Fathers' accomplishments and shortcomings be viewed within the context of their time.[[29]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Founding_Fathers_of_the_United_States#cite_note-29)
## Origin of phrase
[[edit](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Founding_Fathers_of_the_United_States&action=edit&section=2 "Edit section: Origin of phrase")]
The phrase "Founding Fathers" was first introduced to Americans by US senator [Warren G. Harding](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Warren_G._Harding "Warren G. Harding") in his keynote speech at the [Republican National Convention of 1916](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1916_Republican_National_Convention "1916 Republican National Convention"). Harding later repeated the phrase at his [March 4, 1921, presidential inauguration](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Inauguration_of_Warren_G._Harding "Inauguration of Warren G. Harding"), becoming the first president to use the term "Founding Fathers".[[30]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Founding_Fathers_of_the_United_States#cite_note-30) Subsequent speakers adopted the term.[[31]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Founding_Fathers_of_the_United_States#cite_note-31)
The term "fathers" had long been used for the founders. In 1811, responding to praise for his generation, [John Adams](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/John_Adams "John Adams") wrote to a younger [Josiah Quincy III](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Josiah_Quincy_III "Josiah Quincy III"), "I ought not to object to your Reverence for your Fathers as you call them ... but to tell you a very great secret ... I have no reason to believe We were better than you are."[[32]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Founding_Fathers_of_the_United_States#cite_note-32) He also wrote, "Don't call me, ... Father ... [or] Founder ... These titles belong to no man, but to the American people in general."[[33]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Founding_Fathers_of_the_United_States#cite_note-33) In [Thomas Jefferson](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Thomas_Jefferson "Thomas Jefferson")'s [second presidential inaugural address](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Second_inauguration_of_Thomas_Jefferson "Second inauguration of Thomas Jefferson") in 1805, he referred to those who first came to the New World as "forefathers".[[34]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Founding_Fathers_of_the_United_States#cite_note-34) At his [1825 inauguration](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Inauguration_of_John_Quincy_Adams "Inauguration of John Quincy Adams"), [John Quincy Adams](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/John_Quincy_Adams "John Quincy Adams") called the [U.S. Constitution](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Constitution_of_the_United_States "Constitution of the United States") "the work of our forefathers" and expressed his gratitude to "founders of the Union".[[35]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Founding_Fathers_of_the_United_States#cite_note-35)
John Adams and Thomas Jefferson both died on the same day, July 4, 1826. President J. Quincy Adams paid tribute to them as "Fathers" and "Founders of the Republic".[[36]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Founding_Fathers_of_the_United_States#cite_note-36) These terms were used in the U.S. throughout the 19th century, from the inaugurations of [Martin Van Buren](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Martin_Van_Buren "Martin Van Buren") and [James Polk](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/James_K._Polk "James K. Polk") in [1837](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Inauguration_of_Martin_Van_Buren "Inauguration of Martin Van Buren") and [1845](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Inauguration_of_James_K._Polk "Inauguration of James K. Polk"), to [Abraham Lincoln](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Abraham_Lincoln "Abraham Lincoln")'s [Cooper Union speech](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cooper_Union_speech "Cooper Union speech") in 1860 and his [Gettysburg Address](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gettysburg_Address "Gettysburg Address") in 1863, and up to [William McKinley](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/William_McKinley "William McKinley")'s [first inauguration](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/First_inauguration_of_William_McKinley "First inauguration of William McKinley") in 1897.[[37]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Founding_Fathers_of_the_United_States#cite_note-37)[[38]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Founding_Fathers_of_the_United_States#cite_note-38)[[39]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Founding_Fathers_of_the_United_States#cite_note-39)[[40]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Founding_Fathers_of_the_United_States#cite_note-40)
At a 1902 celebration of [Washington's Birthday](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Presidents%27_Day "Presidents' Day") in [Brooklyn](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Brooklyn "Brooklyn"), [James M. Beck](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/James_M._Beck "James M. Beck"), a constitutional lawyer and later a [U.S. congressman](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/United_States_House_of_Representatives "United States House of Representatives"), delivered an address, "Founders of the Republic", in which he connected the concepts of founders and fathers, saying: "It is well for us to remember certain human aspects of the founders of the republic. Let me first refer to the fact that these fathers of the republic were for the most part young men."[[25]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Founding_Fathers_of_the_United_States#cite_note-Beck-25)
## List of Founding Fathers
[[edit](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Founding_Fathers_of_the_United_States&action=edit&section=3 "Edit section: List of Founding Fathers")]
### Framers and signers
[[edit](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Founding_Fathers_of_the_United_States&action=edit&section=4 "Edit section: Framers and signers")]
Further information: [Framers](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Framers "Framers")
[![](https://upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/7/72/Portraits_and_autograph_signatures_of_the_framers_and_signers_of_the_Declaration_of_Independence%2C_Philadelphia%2C_July_4th%2C_1776_%28NYPL_Hades-292367-466033%29.tif/lossy-page1-250px-Portraits_and_autograph_signatures_of_the_framers_and_signers_of_the_Declaration_of_Independence%2C_Philadelphia%2C_July_4th%2C_1776_%28NYPL_Hades-292367-466033%29.tif.jpg)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:Portraits_and_autograph_signatures_of_the_framers_and_signers_of_the_Declaration_of_Independence,_Philadelphia,_July_4th,_1776_\(NYPL_Hades-292367-466033\).tif)Portraits and autograph signatures of the Founding Fathers, [who unanimously signed](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Signing_of_the_United_States_Declaration_of_Independence "Signing of the United States Declaration of Independence") the [Declaration of Independence](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/United_States_Declaration_of_Independence "United States Declaration of Independence") at the [Second Continental Congress](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Second_Continental_Congress "Second Continental Congress") inside present-day [Independence Hall](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Independence_Hall "Independence Hall") in [Philadelphia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Philadelphia "Philadelphia")
The [National Archives](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/National_Archives_and_Records_Administration "National Archives and Records Administration") has identified three founding documents as the "[Charters of Freedom](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Charters_of_Freedom "Charters of Freedom")": Declaration of Independence, [United States Constitution](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/United_States_Constitution "United States Constitution"), and [Bill of Rights](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bill_of_Rights_\(U.S._history\) "Bill of Rights \(U.S. history\)"). According to the archives, these documents "have secured the rights of the American people for nearly two and a half centuries and are considered instrumental to the founding and philosophy of the United States."[[41]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Founding_Fathers_of_the_United_States#cite_note-41) In addition, as the nation's first constitution, the Articles of Confederation and [Perpetual Union](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Perpetual_Union "Perpetual Union") is also a founding document.[[42]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Founding_Fathers_of_the_United_States#cite_note-42)[[43]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Founding_Fathers_of_the_United_States#cite_note-43) As a result, signers of three key documents are generally considered to be Founding Fathers of the United States: Declaration of Independence (DI),[[21]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Founding_Fathers_of_the_United_States#cite_note-ArchivesDeclaration-21) Articles of Confederation (AC),[[23]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Founding_Fathers_of_the_United_States#cite_note-Padover-23) and U.S. Constitution (USC).[[22]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Founding_Fathers_of_the_United_States#cite_note-ArchivesFramers-22) The following table provides a list of these signers, some of whom signed more than one document.

#### Other delegates
[[edit](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Founding_Fathers_of_the_United_States&action=edit&section=5 "Edit section: Other delegates")]
The 55 delegates who attended the [Constitutional Convention](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Constitutional_Convention_\(United_States\) "Constitutional Convention \(United States\)") are referred to as framers. Of these, the 16 listed below did not sign the document.[[44]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Founding_Fathers_of_the_United_States#cite_note-45) Three refused, while the remainder left early, either in protest of the proceedings or for personal reasons.[[45]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Founding_Fathers_of_the_United_States#cite_note-46)[[46]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Founding_Fathers_of_the_United_States#cite_note-47) Nevertheless, some sources regard all framers as founders, including those who did not sign:[[22]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Founding_Fathers_of_the_United_States#cite_note-ArchivesFramers-22)[[47]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Founding_Fathers_of_the_United_States#cite_note-48)
  * [William Richardson Davie](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/William_Richardson_Davie "William Richardson Davie"), North Carolina
  * [Oliver Ellsworth](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Oliver_Ellsworth "Oliver Ellsworth"), Connecticut
  * [Elbridge Gerry](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Elbridge_Gerry "Elbridge Gerry"), Massachusetts*
  * [William Houston](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/William_Houston "William Houston"), New Jersey
  * [William Houstoun](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/William_Houstoun_\(lawyer\) "William Houstoun \(lawyer\)"), Georgia
  * [John Lansing Jr.](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/John_Lansing_Jr. "John Lansing Jr."), New York
  * [Alexander Martin](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Alexander_Martin "Alexander Martin"), North Carolina
  * [Luther Martin](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Luther_Martin "Luther Martin"), Maryland
  * [George Mason](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/George_Mason "George Mason"), Virginia*
  * [James McClurg](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/James_McClurg "James McClurg"), Virginia
  * [John Francis Mercer](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/John_Francis_Mercer "John Francis Mercer"), Maryland
  * [William Pierce](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/William_Pierce_\(Georgia_politician\) "William Pierce \(Georgia politician\)"), Georgia
  * [Edmund Randolph](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Edmund_Randolph "Edmund Randolph"), Virginia*
  * [Caleb Strong](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Caleb_Strong "Caleb Strong"), Massachusetts
  * [George Wythe](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/George_Wythe "George Wythe"), Virginia
  * [Robert Yates](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Robert_Yates_\(politician\) "Robert Yates \(politician\)"), New York


(*) Randolph, Mason, and Gerry were the only three present at the Constitution's adoption who refused to sign.
### Additional Founding Fathers
[[edit](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Founding_Fathers_of_the_United_States&action=edit&section=6 "Edit section: Additional Founding Fathers")]
In addition to the signers and Framers of the founding documents and one of the seven notable leaders previously mentioned—[John Jay](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/John_Jay "John Jay")—the following are regarded as founders based on their contributions to the creation and early development of the new nation: 
  * [Elias Boudinot](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Elias_Boudinot "Elias Boudinot"), New Jersey representative in the Continental Congress, [Congress of the Confederation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Congress_of_the_Confederation "Congress of the Confederation") (president 1782–1783), and the first three [U.S. Congresses](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/United_States_Congress "United States Congress"). Boudinot was director of the [U.S. Mint](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/United_States_Mint "United States Mint") under presidents Washington, Adams, and Jefferson, and also was the founding president of the [American Bible Society](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/American_Bible_Society "American Bible Society").[[48]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Founding_Fathers_of_the_United_States#cite_note-auto10-49)
  * [George Clinton](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/George_Clinton_\(vice_president\) "George Clinton \(vice president\)"), first governor of New York, 1777–1795, served again from 1801 to 1805, and was the fourth vice president of the US, 1805–1812. He was an anti-Federalist advocate of the Bill of Rights.[[49]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Founding_Fathers_of_the_United_States#cite_note-berstein179-50)
  * [Patrick Henry](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Patrick_Henry "Patrick Henry"), gifted orator, known for his famous quote, "[Give me liberty or give me death!](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Give_me_liberty_or_give_me_death! "Give me liberty or give me death!")",[[50]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Founding_Fathers_of_the_United_States#cite_note-51) served in the First Continental Congress in 1774 and briefly in the Second Congress in 1775 before returning to Virginia to lead its militia. He then completed terms as the first and sixth governor of Virginia, 1776–1779 and 1784–1786.[[51]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Founding_Fathers_of_the_United_States#cite_note-52)
  * [Esek Hopkins](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Esek_Hopkins "Esek Hopkins"), commander-in-chief of the [Continental Navy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Continental_Navy "Continental Navy")[[52]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Founding_Fathers_of_the_United_States#cite_note-53)
  * [Henry Knox](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Henry_Knox "Henry Knox") served as chief [artillery](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Artillery "Artillery") officer in most of Washington's campaigns. His earliest achievement was the capture of over 50 pieces of artillery, primarily cannons, at New York's Fort Ticonderoga, one of the keys to Washington's capture of Boston in early 1776. Knox became the first [secretary of war](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Secretary_of_War "Secretary of War") under the U.S. Constitution in 1789.[[25]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Founding_Fathers_of_the_United_States#cite_note-Beck-25)
  * [Gilbert du Motier, Marquis de Lafayette](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Marquis_de_Lafayette "Marquis de Lafayette"), French Marquis who became a Continental Army general.[[53]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Founding_Fathers_of_the_United_States#cite_note-54) Served without pay, brought a ship to America, outfitted for war, provided clothing and other provisions for the patriot cause, all at his own expense.[[54]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Founding_Fathers_of_the_United_States#cite_note-55)
  * [Arthur Lee](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Arthur_Lee_\(diplomat\) "Arthur Lee \(diplomat\)"), diplomat who helped negotiate and signed the 1778 [Treaty of Alliance](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Treaty_of_Alliance_\(1778\) "Treaty of Alliance \(1778\)") with France, along with Benjamin Franklin and Silas Deane.
  * [Robert R. Livingston](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Robert_R._Livingston "Robert R. Livingston"), member of the [Committee of Five](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Committee_of_Five "Committee of Five") that drafted the Declaration of Independence, 1776; first [U.S. secretary of foreign affairs](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/United_States_Secretary_of_Foreign_Affairs "United States Secretary of Foreign Affairs"), 1781–1783, and first chancellor of New York, 1777–1801. He administered the [presidential oath of office](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Oath_of_office_of_the_President_of_the_United_States "Oath of office of the President of the United States") at the [first inauguration of George Washington](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/First_inauguration_of_George_Washington "First inauguration of George Washington") and with James Monroe negotiated the [Louisiana Purchase](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Louisiana_Purchase "Louisiana Purchase") as the minister to France.[[55]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Founding_Fathers_of_the_United_States#cite_note-56)[[56]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Founding_Fathers_of_the_United_States#cite_note-57)
  * [John Marshall](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/John_Marshall "John Marshall") served with George Washington at Valley Forge and later would be the first to refer to him as "the Father of his country". Appointed the fourth chief justice of the U.S. Supreme Court under John Adams, Marshall defined the authority of the court and ensured the stability of the federal government during the first three decades of the 19th century.[[57]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Founding_Fathers_of_the_United_States#cite_note-bernstein180-58)[[58]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Founding_Fathers_of_the_United_States#cite_note-LOCk-o-59)[[59]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Founding_Fathers_of_the_United_States#cite_note-60)[[60]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Founding_Fathers_of_the_United_States#cite_note-61)
  * [James Monroe](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/James_Monroe "James Monroe"), elected to the Virginia legislature (1782); member of the Continental Congress (1783–1786);[[61]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Founding_Fathers_of_the_United_States#cite_note-62) fifth president of the United States for two terms (1817–1825);[[62]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Founding_Fathers_of_the_United_States#cite_note-63) Negotiated the Louisiana Purchase along with Robert Livingston.[[63]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Founding_Fathers_of_the_United_States#cite_note-64)
  * [James Otis Jr.](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/James_Otis_Jr. "James Otis Jr."), pamphleteer, one of the earliest proponents of patriotic causes, an opponent of slavery, and leader of Massachusetts' Committee of Correspondence, all in the 1760s.[[64]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Founding_Fathers_of_the_United_States#cite_note-65)[[65]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Founding_Fathers_of_the_United_States#cite_note-66)
  * [Thomas Paine](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Thomas_Paine "Thomas Paine"), author of _[Common Sense](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Common_Sense "Common Sense")_ and other influential pamphlets in the 1770s; sometimes referred to as "Father of the American Revolution".[[57]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Founding_Fathers_of_the_United_States#cite_note-bernstein180-58)[[66]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Founding_Fathers_of_the_United_States#cite_note-67)[[67]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Founding_Fathers_of_the_United_States#cite_note-68) While John Adams strongly criticized Paine for failing to see the need for a separation of powers in government, _Common Sense_ proved crucial in building support for independence following its publication in January 1776.[[68]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Founding_Fathers_of_the_United_States#cite_note-69)[[69]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Founding_Fathers_of_the_United_States#cite_note-70)
  * [Timothy Pickering](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Timothy_Pickering "Timothy Pickering"), Secretary of War, [U.S. secretary of state](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/United_States_Secretary_of_State "United States Secretary of State"), from Massachusetts. Fired by President John Adams; replaced by John Marshall.[[70]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Founding_Fathers_of_the_United_States#cite_note-71)
  * [Thomas Pinckney](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Thomas_Pinckney "Thomas Pinckney"), signer of the [Pinckney's Treaty](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pinckney%27s_Treaty "Pinckney's Treaty") with Spain, fought as an officer in the [Continental Army](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Continental_Army "Continental Army") and served as a [United States ambassador to Great Britain](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/United_States_Ambassador_to_Great_Britain "United States Ambassador to Great Britain")
  * [Peyton Randolph](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Peyton_Randolph "Peyton Randolph"), speaker of Virginia's [House of Burgesses](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/House_of_Burgesses "House of Burgesses"), [president of the First Continental Congress](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Presidents_of_the_Continental_Congress "Presidents of the Continental Congress"), and a signer of the Continental Association.[[71]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Founding_Fathers_of_the_United_States#cite_note-72)
  * [John Rogers](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/John_Rogers_\(Continental_Congress\) "John Rogers \(Continental Congress\)"), Maryland lawyer and judge, delegate to the [Continental Congress](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Continental_Congress "Continental Congress") who voted for the Declaration of Independence but fell ill before he could sign it.[[72]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Founding_Fathers_of_the_United_States#cite_note-73)
  * [Charles Thomson](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Charles_Thomson "Charles Thomson"), secretary of the Continental Congress from its formation to its final session, 1774–1789.[[73]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Founding_Fathers_of_the_United_States#cite_note-74)
  * [Joseph Warren](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Joseph_Warren "Joseph Warren"), respected physician and architect of the Revolutionary movement, known as the "Founding Martyr" for his death at the [Battle of Bunker Hill](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Battle_of_Bunker_Hill "Battle of Bunker Hill"), drafted the [Suffolk Resolves](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Suffolk_Resolves "Suffolk Resolves") in response to the [Intolerable Acts](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Intolerable_Acts "Intolerable Acts").[[74]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Founding_Fathers_of_the_United_States#cite_note-cary19-20-75)
  * ["Mad Anthony" Wayne](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Anthony_Wayne "Anthony Wayne"), a prominent army general during the Revolutionary War.[[57]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Founding_Fathers_of_the_United_States#cite_note-bernstein180-58)[[75]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Founding_Fathers_of_the_United_States#cite_note-Buchanan-76)
  * [Thomas Willing](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Thomas_Willing "Thomas Willing"), delegate to the [Continental Congress](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Continental_Congress "Continental Congress") from Pennsylvania, the first president of the [Bank of North America](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bank_of_North_America "Bank of North America"), and the first president of the [First Bank of the United States](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/First_Bank_of_the_United_States "First Bank of the United States")[[76]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Founding_Fathers_of_the_United_States#cite_note-77)
  * [Henry Wisner](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Henry_Wisner "Henry Wisner"), New York Continental Congress delegate who voted for the Declaration of Independence but left Philadelphia before the signing.
